So, Ta'ala al-Amal is dangerous. Now, there's a tradition. It's not a hadith, but it's often quoted as a hadith.

And the tradition says that, I'amal li dunyaka ka'anaka ta'ishu abada. wa'anma li ukhraka ka'anaka tamut ughada. Act for your dunya as if you'll live forever and act for your Aqara as if you're going to die tomorrow.

Now, Shaykh Basheer, Asman Basheer, Rahimullah, a dear friend of mine who was, lived in Medina most of his life. He was from Uritriya. He told me once, he said, you know, people don't understand that saying.

And he said, it's always used as a justification for working really hard for dunya. So, the dunya, when people love that hadith, because they always say, ya'akhi, a'amal li dunyaka ka'anaka ta'ishu abada. He said, if you look at it in the context of work for your dunya as if you'll live forever, but work for your Aqara as if you're going to die tomorrow.

He said, if you then look at your situation, bifocally, one, your dunya matters, you have forever to take care of them, but your Aqara, you're going to die tomorrow. He said, you're going to start preparing for your Aqara and say, I have plenty of time to take care of my dunya. So, it's the opposite kind of understanding that most people have, and that's really the way.

Now, the other thing is, that's insan al siva kaminat dunya, in the Quran, karon, don't forget your portion of the dunya. There's two ways that's interpreted. One is, don't forget what Allah's given you in order that you can expend it for Aqara.

And the other is, the dunya is there and Allah gave it to you. So, don't forget that there is dunya, but the important thing is Aqara. And these are both, they're both acceptable positions.

So, we're not anti-worldly, like say, for instance, traditional Christianity was. We're not against the world, you know. The world is a place Allah made it for us to enjoy as well, but not to enjoy it to the point where we forget what we're really here for and that it's a bridge.

And then he says, and then another, the verse in the Quran in which Allah Subh'anaHu Wa Ta-A'la says, and this is an example of it, he said, A long time passed and their hearts became hard. So, the idea is that if you're not really thinking about Aqara, the longer you are on the earth, the harder the heart gets. Now, if you look at children, one of the really fascinating things about children, they can cry like that, very easily.

And part of it is that their hearts are very supple, and they go into several states in one day. They can go from extreme joy to extreme sadness in one day, right? And it's very interesting why their hearts are like that. They're very pure.

And so, everything affects them in very profound ways. And as a person gets older, it becomes more and more difficult for them. And this is why, one of the things, why the Prophet SallAllahu Alaihi Wasallam loved women, one of the things that he loved from the world or was made to love was women, and the Ullama say, liraqati qurubihina, because their hearts are more brittle than men.

Right? Their hearts are more brittle than men. In other words, women have more compassion, their hearts are more open, things like that. For men, it's very easy for that heart to become hard.

And so, the danger of poul al-amad is that it makes the hard heart. And then he says, laki nuhufihaqri min nirad an'ab, aukan ifi tarsniki ar-inmanlam yu'ab. Regarding the one who's engaged in preparing for tomorrow or writing works of knowledge, I mean, obviously he puts that in there.

He's a scholar, and that's one of the highest things that you can do is, sadaqajaryah, or ilm yun tafar bihi nas, like ongoing charity, or knowledge that people will benefit. So he's saying, if you're engaged in benefiting people with knowledge like writing books, then it's not a blameworthy thing to want to live a long life. And the Prophet SallAllahu Alaihi Wasallam said, that's a taman no almo, don't desire to die.

Because he said, if you did wrong in the past, you want life to make toba and rectify what you did wrong. Or if you did good, you want more time to do more good. So either way, even if your life has been bad in the past, each day is a new day and a new opportunity to make toba to Allah and to change your ways.

So you should always desire long life if you're engaged in good things. If you're not, then what he's saying is, it's a disaster. So for people who are not wasting their time and recognize the urgency of the human condition and their own impending immortality and sets out doing useful deeds, such as spreading knowledge or anything else that's useful.

And Allah Subh'anaHu Wa Ta-A'la says that, that what benefits, right, yam kuthufir arb, it continues and it goes on in the earth. And that's why Abu Bakr is the most generous of all of the Sahaba. He gave everything when Ummah only gave half. Only gave half.

So another virtue. This one, they call them the ummahats, the matrices of virtue. These are the matrices.

All the virtues emerge from these four. Mercy, right? So if we look at mercy, what is mercy? Mercy is not Adam. Yeah, it would be, mercy would be hikma.

Because if you look at compassion and mercy, for instance, Abu Bakr, when he told the Prophet to be merciful to the people, what was really behind it? The hikma, that Allah gives with mercy what he doesn't give with wrath. So there's a wisdom in showing mercy, right? When you show mercy, and it comes from a knowledge that, and you can do that with any of the virtues. You can find where they lie in one of these four.

And it's been done. You can exhaust yourself thinking about it, but some of the greatest minds that ever existed in human history, they really exhausted themselves. Imam al-Ghazadi goes into great detail in the ikhya of showing how all the virtues come from these four.

And he really exhausted it. So now, how does this all relate to rabab? Rabab, because it's one of these kawas, it's coming from something that's essential to the human nature, right? If you look at the brain, the way the brain has been made, right? They call the human brain a triune brain because it has three dominant centers. And this is also not so modern.

I mean, this is actually, Imam al-Ghazadi does mention this. The lowest brain, they call it the R brain, which is really out of evolutionary theory, the reptilian brain. And this is the appetitive center of the brain.

It functions, these basic functions, the drive, the urges of the human being, the food urge, the sex urge, all these urges. And then there's the midbrain. So this would relate to the appetitive.

And then the midbrain is where the emotions are centered. And from this model, the root emotion is rabab. And this is why children, the real first emotions that they display are rabab.

They get angry, right? And they express the anger through crying and as they get older through tantrum or they always learn that before they learn naan, right? They learn no before they learn yes. And you can see it rooted in that. So this brain is related to the emotions.

And this is why there's a beautiful hadith in Imam At-Kirmidhi in which the Prophet said, He said, He said, love the one you love up to a point because one day he might hate you. And the wisdom here is if you tell somebody all your secrets and then one day they turn on you, which is a human experience, it occurs quite often. And people are often devastated by that.

And also don't hate people extremely because one day you might end up being people that love each other. So be balanced in your love and in your hate. And so this is again an indication of having a balance of emotions.

And when they get out of balance, people become, you know, if you have too much love, one of the Arabs, he said, Don't be sukkar and get eaten up. And don't be callousynth and get thrown away. You know, don't be too sweet because they'll eat you alive.

And don't be too bitter because you won't have any friends. So be balanced. And that's the idea.

And then finally, what they call the new brain is the neocortex, which is where this would be the rational. When these three are functioning properly, the human being is Adam. Right? In other words, and see, this is part of the human condition.

You have people that are excessively cerebral. They're completely in their heads. And it's everything's theoretical to them.

There's nothing practical. They go to this extreme. Then you have other people that are like reptiles out there.

They're literally living like a reptile. And I explained this once in a prison. And some of these people, the Muslim inmates really responded to this.

And one came up to me afterwards. He said, you know that thing about the reptilian brain? He said, there's a lot of reptiles here. He said, these guys are like snakes just waiting to attack you.

It's an interesting phenomenon, this idea of people in that really completely reactionary mode. There's no humanity there. And then you have people that are, obviously you see them overly emotional.

They're either angry, right? And that's the more common one, to get people that are very angry. So now, I just brought all of that in there to put this into perspective. Anger is one of the excesses of the human condition, which is why he calls it the ocean.

Because it really is the big one. And there's basically three reasons why people get angry. One of them relates to primal needs.

Food, shelter, life, those type of things. That's the first type of... Now this is Mejbul. Yeah, and he's a human being.

If he doesn't get angry for this, he's considered Ahmuk. He goes under the category of stupidity. Because this is a survival.

If somebody's threatening your life, you have to respond to it, right? I mean, you need to respond to it. If somebody is threatening your family, you need to respond to that. That is a Khabib that Allah loves.

And Allah loves Shuja'a, wadauf yukhatiri haya. Even in killing a snake. And also the Prophet SallAllahu Alaihi Wasallam said, manmata duna madihi, manmata shahida.

Anyone that dies defending his wealth dies a shahid. So that Khabib, and because part of it is, you don't want people to think it's easy in a society to rob people. You don't, I mean, you're acting, doing that as a type, it's an ultimate amar-bir ma'arauf and nahi'an in munkar.

And if you die doing it, you died in jihad. Because amar-bir ma'arauf and nahi'an in munkar, it's in babble jihad. It's a type of jihad.

So, the second type is also, and this is common to human beings, the second type is also common to human beings. But this relates to position, right? Or it relates to things that have to do with ru'ryat and nafs, seeing yourself, right? In other words, I have dignity. He can't do this to me, right? I'm a human being.

And this kind of anger that people get angry about that relates to a sense of belittlement. Somebody is belittling them. Somebody is treating them with disdain.

Somebody is treating them less than what they want. And they could be right, they could be wrong. It just depends.

Because some people have a completely, they have a complete delusion about who they are, right? A delusion of the grandeur of their own soul. And so they actually... And then finally, the third one relates to specific people. This is a specific one.

And this relates to, for instance, an anim, if he sees a book being mistreated, will get angry. But the farmer who's illiterate, he won't get angry. Because he doesn't really understand, you know, what's the big deal.

He sat on a book, so what? Whereas the anim sees it, that's wrong because it's related to his. Now, the farmer will get angry if you throw the pitchfork, you know, because it's his tool. What are you doing? You know, you're going to break that, and he'll get upset at that.

So this is a type of anger that relates to specific people, right? And these three are why people get angry. Now, Imam al-Ghazadi says the first one is healthy, unless it's taken to an extreme where you go and oppress people, take their food, take their shelter, do things like that. That's oppression.

But the next one, he says, it can be healthy up to a point. But the higher way is to stop seeing yourself and not get angry about these things. And the danger of it is you go to an extreme which is called ba'a, and he'll deal with that.

It's a disease, which is where you become so humble that you become humiliated. And the Prophet SallAllahu Alaihi Wasallam says, al-Mukminu layudilunafsa, the Mukman does not humiliate himself. And Allah says that there is an al-Kufar, ruhama'u bainohum.

They have izzah when it comes to the non-Muslims. You're not all humble and meek and Uriah Heath obsequiousness. You have izzah with the kafir.

He shouldn't see you as being humble and weak like that, in the face of him. Whereas with the Mu'lman, you should. You should give up those things.

And even according to Sidi Ahmad Zarrokh, if the kafir says something to you that does not relate to your deen, like not making fun of your deen or something, then you should just ignore it. izzah katabohum al-jahidoona karu salaam. When ignorant people speak to them, they just say salaam.

You shouldn't get angry for things that really, there's no benefit in getting angry, because all they're doing is pushing buttons. And that's why Allah says, Just be patient about what they're saying and then leave them in a good way. So just turn away from it.

Don't obsess yourself with it and let it go, because there's no benefit in it. No benefit. And then finally, the fourth one, again, if there's ghayra, ghayra is something that Sa'ad Rabbilah Anu when the Prophet SallAllahu Alaihi Wasallam was mentioning about the wife, and Sa'ad said, And the Prophet SallAllahu Alaihi Wasallam said, I have more ghayra than Sa'ad.

And Allah has more ghayra than I do. In other words, that is a good sign. Ghayra is a good thing.

And they say, ghayra, sometimes it's translated as jealousy. It's an idea of something that you are in protection of or something that's yours, that not letting somebody else do harm to it. A ghayra takes you.

So this is why when a Muslim sees the non-Muslim threatening his deen, he gets a ghayra. He becomes ghayr for the deen. He wants to defend the deen.

And that's a good thing. That's an anger that's positive. And this is why Allah has made ghayra part of the human nature to protect Nisib.

Because if men didn't have ghayra for their women, they would lose Nisib. People would go and commit adultery with their women and then you would have a mixture of the Nisib. So ghayra is a healthy thing for a man to have.

To be jealous of his wife is a good thing as long as it doesn't go to, obviously, an excess where he oppresses her and becomes suspicious. And it's also a natural thing for the woman to have that ghayra, right? I mean, Allah has made Mubarak. He permitted the marriage of more than one wife.

But the actual experience of a woman towards the man of ghayra is natural. It's part of human nature. And very few women are free from that.

So it's not considered a fault, right? Right? Now he says here, He says that this disease has two cures. One of them will remove the anger when it comes, right? So that you prevent it from coming. And the second will suppress the anger.

Now Allah says in the Quran, He praises, Those who suppress their anger, and those who forgive people. And that's the famous story of Jafar al-Payar when one of his servants was pouring and spilled water on him, he became angry, and the servant immediately said, And he said, You know, I suppressed it. And he said, And he said, You know, I forgive you.

And he said, And he said, He said, You're free for the sake of Allah. And that's the way the moment should be. He should be somebody when he hears the Quran.

One of the things that has been pointed out is the idea of the mean. What's called al-wasyapa in Arabic. Al-wasyapa is the mean.

And, one of the things, the dangers of virtues... Is that virtues are means between extremes. With the exception of the extreme. With the exception of justice, which is the opposite of injustice.

So, it has an opposite, but it doesn't have extremes. Now, the next disease that he talks about is a disease that is an extreme. The virtue is humility.

Some might argue, actually, in Christianity that it is a type of virtue. But, in Islam, it's definitely not a virtue. So, he calls it a dhul.

So, the sun never sets on the British Empire, and then it sets. And then, the British Empire, the lion of Great Britain, becomes the tail of America. So, that's a humiliation for the Anglo-Saxons of Britain, who have always seen the Americans as the colonies.

So, al-mu'minu la yudhillu nafsa. The mu'min never humiliates himself. He never abases himself.

You don't humiliate yourself. You humble yourself. And, so he says, adhulla wa ba'ata.

Ba'a is to be low. And, the Prophet ﷺ said, Nothing raises itself, illa wa ba'ahu Allah. Except Allah will abase it.

That's the sunnah of Allah and His creation. If you raise yourself, Allah will abase you. And, so that's really important.

Because, these are wisdoms, you know. And, now, with Allah, you should have dhul. With Allah, you should be dhalil.

Before Allah, subhanahu wa ta'ala. And, that's why, Sidi Abu Qadir al-Jilani said, He saw all of the doors to Allah. And, they were all crowded, except for one.

Dhul wa tawad'u. The door of humility and humbleness. And, so, he said to people, Halumma ir'a rabbikum.

You know, come, you want to get to your Lord? This is the door. Because, nobody is there. So, it's hard on the nafs.

There's other ones that they mentioned. But, al ghani al mustakbir is the norm. Rich people.

The second person is ibadah. The next disease is, karahat al-dhim. Dhim is blame.

Insan, it means to blame him. You know, ahummah. Dhim is to blame.

The nature of the human being is, he does not like to be blamed. That is part of our nature. It's inherent in the human being.

We don't like to be blamed. We teach it to our children early on. We praise them for things.

And, we blame them for other things. And, they start seeing that the praise is nice. Because, it goes with it.

Smiles, gifts, ah, love, hugs, whatever. And, the blame is painful. Because, usually, it goes with that.

Ah, frowns, anger, raised voices, sometimes, ah, physical abuse. These type things. So, we teach early on, ah, children about praise and blame.

And, they learn very quickly to want praise and to, to dislike blame. The problem is, is that, it becomes a disease. Karahiyatu dhim.

Because, there's a lot to be learned, ah, first of all, in them. But, also, ah, some other things, which he's going to clarify here. He says, karahatu dhimmi banan ma'lufu.

It's a well-known disease. Wannathuru al'ibadi walwukufu ma'hum. Hijabun an maqam al-ihsan.

So, what he's saying is, displeasure with blame is a well-known disease of the heart. Concern with creation's opinions and desiring their praise and displeasure with their criticism becomes a barrier between you and the station of ihsan. Because, what you start doing is you start doing your actions in order to be blamed or praised.

And, one of the things in the seerah class, Sa'd ibn Mu'adh, radhi Allah anhu, when they bring him to do, the Prophet says to the Aus, will you accept, because the Bani Quraidah were confederates of the Aus. Right? I mean, it's all relative, but the fact that they were joining the army. Bani Nadir were attempting to assassinate the Prophet, which is actually worse. You know, in the big picture of things.

But, the point is is that Sa'd ibn Mu'adh said a really important thing. When he came out, they were telling him, please you know, have the Prophet forgive him, just like the Bani Nadir got off. And, he said, I have reached a point where in my mawqif, I have no fear for the I'm not worried. 

He was about to die. He said, I'm not in a position to be worried about what people are going to say. See, because what Aus was worried about was, what are people going to say? These are our confederates and if they all get killed, they'll say the Khazraj got their confederates off, but the Aus didn't get their confederates off.

You see? So, they were thinking about what people are going to say. Sa'd ibn Mu'adh said, I'm not worried about, I'm about to meet my Lord. I'm not concerned about what people are going to say.

So, he said, al-ana you know, now wajittu nafsi fi maqamin laa khafu fihi laumata la'im. I found myself in a place, which is right before death. I'm not worried about what people are going to say.

The point of that is, the reality of it is, you are in that moment at every instant of your life. You know, death is, it's just a veil to think that you, that's thool al-amal, which is another disease. It's a veil from Allah to make you think, oh, I'm going to you know, be around.

It's a complete illusion. You have no guarantees whatsoever. And so, what this is saying is, Ihsan is, that's where Sa'd was. 

He was in maqam al-ihsan. All he could see was Allah. He wasn't concerned about creation.

And that's the point with this karahiyat al-dham. You start worrying about what other people are going to think, and that will often prevent you from doing the right thing, because very often the right thing is what everybody else is going to be against. And that's the nature of the world. 

So, taking the right position often means taking the position that you will be blamed for by others for doing it. And very often, courageous stances, they become known later on. I mean, for instance, Geronimo is a good example.

No one. No human being has those attributes. It's only Allah that can benefit, and it's only Allah that can harm. 

So if you're worried about what people think, and it's preventing you from doing what you should do for the sake of Allah, then you're not seeing reality. You're deluded. He was in the hospital in Jeddah, and he had cancer, and he was going to die, and he had almost no strength, and they had him hooked up to all this stuff, and then he was there for a while, and then one day, he told his son, we have to go to Medina today, and he said, the doctor said, no, no, he can't leave, and he said, no, I'm leaving today, they're going to drive me, my son will drive me, and I'm leaving the hospital, and his doctor was totally, said, no, you're too sick, and you need to stay here. He said, no. They took him that night, he went all the way back, got to Medina, he did, just said, La ilaha illallah, the whole night, right before Fajr, he told his son to say a few things to certain people, and this and that, and then he said the Shahadah, and died.

He was buried in Baqiyah, and the place he was buried, when they went in, he gave me a piece of the, from the road that covers the Prophet's grave, because they cut it up when they changed it, and he gave me a piece of that, but he, and he was buried with that. But he, the place that he was buried is right next to Imam Marik and Nafia, where people aren't buried anymore, because it's in the old Baqiyah. But when they came in, they found there was a dug grave there, and so they just, they put him in that grave.

But he was prayed on, on Jum'ah, he was the only person that died that day, or that was, had Janazah that day at the Prophet's Mosque. So that, I mean, I'm just telling you that story, because that is an example of how you know, Muslims who know how to die, die. I mean, there's a story, Allah-u-An'am, of it's truth, but it's, you know, it's a story told in Persia about Faridur-in-al-Ataar, who was a perfumer.

And he actually tells a story, I think, that he, the way that he made his tawbah to Allah was a man once came begging for alms, and he told him, like, why don't you get a job? And he said, this is my job. And he said, what do you do? And he said, well, what do you do? He said, I sell perfumes. And he said, the most important thing that you're ever going to do is die.

And he said, and I know how to do that. Ash-shadu an-la ilaha illallah, ash-shadu an-mahlum bi-rasulillah, and he died. So, there are people that do know how to die, you know.

And whether that story's true or not, there's a truth in it. Huh? Yeah. I mean, there really is, there are people that know how to die.

And when the time comes, they will know what to do. And there's other people that don't. And when the time comes, they go into a real state of confusion.

So, and that's one of the blessings also of doing dhikr of death. And another thing that our scholars have recommended and the Prophet sallallahu alaihi wa sallam, based on his hadith of remembering death, is to go through the states of the akhira. To actually go through visualization of dying, of being washed, of being wrapped, of being put into your grave, of having the dirt cover you, and then being asked by the angels, and then resurrection.

Of actually going through the states and the mizan. To reflect on those things as a practice to do. It's a very sobering type of practice to do that.

Just to really get you out of the sleep that we're in, in life. Now, another thing he says here is that, أَمَّ إِذَا قَلَاهُ لَا لِذَاتِهِ وَلَا لَلْإِنْسَرَامِ عَنْ لَذَّاتِهِ بَالْخَوْفَ قَطْعِهِ عَنْ إِسْتِعْدَادِهِ بِطَاعَةِ اللَّهِ إِلَىٰ مَعَادِهِ If he doesn't like death, not for its death, because it's death, nor because it's going to cut him off from the pleasures of dunya, but if he doesn't like it out of fear that he hasn't had enough time to prepare for death, then that's a good reason. And the Prophet ﷺ encouraged you to desire a long life, because he said, if you're a bad person, you have time to make up for the wrong you've done, and if you're a good person, you have more time to increase your good, and to strengthen your صحيفة, or your scroll on the يوم القيامة.

So, we should have, and he mentioned that about طول الأمد, that it was good to desire, if you were in good deeds, it was a good thing to desire a long life for that reason. And the Prophet ﷺ, about death, he once said to Aisha رضي الله عنه من أحب الله أحب لقاءه Whoever loves Allah loves to meet Allah, and she said, يا رسول الله الموت نكره الموت We don't like death. And the Prophet ﷺ said that that was normal.

That was a natural thing. But rather, it was a dislike of the akhirah, of meeting Allah. You see.

So, the point is, is that the dislike of death is obviously natural. I mean, Allah has put it in our body. It's at a cellular level, that you don't want to die, right? And that's why, in certain instances where your life's threatened, you go into a hyper state of functioning, and you'll do things that are really not at the conscious level to protect yourself or others from death.

Right? So, that is normal, and Allah has put that in us. That's a good thing, and that's why Ibrahim ﷺ when he said, أَلَا تَأْكُلُونَ when he offered them the food, the angels, and they didn't eat, فَأَوْجَسَ مِنْهُمْ خِيْفًا You know, he felt fear, and that fear was that they were going to rob him and kill him, or something. So, that's a natural thing to have that.

And that's not what he's talking about there. And then he says, أَوْفَوَّضَ الْأَمْرَ إِلَى مَوْلَاهُ فَمَا يَشَٓى أَرْضَهُ أَوَبْقَاهُ If on the other hand, also, if he completely entrusted his affair to his master, and whatever he wills, either felling him or giving him respite, contents him. So, in other words, you don't mind. 

Whatever Allah wants to do with you, you're just content with that. That's fine. And so, both those positions are good positions.

And that's why he says, فَذَانِ مَمْدُحَانِ مَحْمُدَانِ وَالْكُرْهُ لَا يُغْعِضُ مِنْ كَدَّانِ He says that both those attitudes towards death are commendable and praiseworthy in any way. A dislike of the reality of death in no way distances you from your death's proximity. So, even if you don't like death, it doesn't, you know, it's not going to do anything.

It's not going to make it any more distant from you. It's much better to accept it for your own state. And the other thing that's important about this is that one of the, well, he mentions it here.

And then he says ذَاكِرُهُ يُكْرَمُ بِالْقَنَاعَ وَبِنَشَاطِ قَلْبِهِ لَطَاعَ The one who remembers death much, he's ennobled by certain characteristics, four things. One of them is قَنَاعَ Because of your remembrance of death, you tend to be more content with what you have. In other words, if you really think that you could die anytime, there's going to be more of an impetus to enjoy enjoy the experience of life now instead of always thinking, you know, eventually I'm going to get it together as they say in America.

And that never happens, right? So the point is قَنَاعَ And this is the Prophet ﷺ said القَنَاعَةُ كَنزٌ لَا يَفْنَى Contentment is a treasure that is never exhausted. So قَنَاعَ is really important. And the Prophet ﷺ said اللَّهُمَّ أَرْزُقَ آلَ مُحَمَّدَنَا القَنَاعَ وَالكَفَافِ Oh Allah, give the family of my family the family of the Prophet ﷺ contentment and enough to live their lives with.

So just give them enough to get by and then make them content with it. And that's why the قَنُوع, a person who's in a state of قَنَاعَ, he's the only wealthy person. So a poor man whose content is a rich man in reality.

A rich man whose discontent is a poor man in reality. Because فَقَر is want. And قَنَاعَ is you have no want. 

If you don't have want, you're rich. Because the definition of a rich person is that without need. So if you don't have need, you're wealthy.

And that's why الغينة غين النفس The Prophet ﷺ said real wealth is the wealth of the soul. Right? الغينة ليس عن كثرة العرض It's not having a lot of stuff. وإنما الغينة غين النفس It's the contentment of the soul.

And so when you remember death, Allah, one of the blessings, He's telling you this is a given, you're given قَنَاعَ. And this is an age where people, there's so many false needs and wants that are inculcated in the hearts of people. I mean that's what media is doing constantly, just يَغْرِس You know, it's just planting the seeds of فَمَع, of desire, of شَهْوَة, of all these things in the heart.

And then He says, it also will give you energy, right? His heart's activity is directed towards obedience. Right? So, by remembering death, it will give you a type of نشاط You become نشط, which is to be energized, to do good deeds because if you think about death, then it's going to make you want to be more obedient to Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَ تَعَالَى. وَبِبِدَارِي تَوْبَةٍ وَيُبْتَرَى.

And then also, the other thing that you're given is you're given a a state where you'll hurry to تَوْبَة. If something does occur, if a slip does happen and you do something wrong, it will cause you to quickly make تَوْبَة, which is rectifying things. So تَوْبَة rectifies wrong actions and that's a gift of remembrance of death because if you do something wrong and then you say سُبْحَانَ اللَّهُ, I'm accountable for this. 

أَسْتَغْفِرُ اللَّهُ. Then it causes you to actually ask Allah سُبْحَانَهُ وَ تَعَالَى forgiveness. Now, the opposite is true. 

The one who forgets his death, he's given those three opposites. He will always want more. He'll never have قَنَاعًا.

And he's also going to be يُرْسِلُ جَوَارِحَهُ فِي المَعْصِيَة. He'll do wrong actions because he's not worried about death. He's not thinking about death and the يوم القيامة and the حساب and these things.

And then finally, he won't have remorse when he does things that are wrong because he's not really worried about what's going to happen in the Akhirah. Protection, taking care of the needs of the person, etc. etc. So, the next disease is called oblivion to blessings.

In other words, الرَّحْمَانِ the blessings of الرَّحْمَانِ are easy. Like health, it's obvious. If all you have to do is say to somebody Oh, thank God you're healthy and they say you're right, you know.

Your taste buds. That's a ne'am. Allah could have made your food rocks and He could have made your water brackish.

But He made it sweet. He could have made all the foods taste the same. But He gave like lemons for people who like sour things.

And honey for people who like sweet. He gave all these different things. And then He gave you different taste buds.

To where you could actually enjoy the different things in it. So, all of these, this idea of reflecting on the ne'am. One of the things Imam al-Ghazali says, he said, look at your food.

Allah says that in the Quran. فَلِنظُرْ الْإِنسَانُ إِلَىٰ طَعَامِ إِنَّا صَبَبْنَا مَا أَصَبَّ ثُمَّ شَقَقْنَا الْأَرْضَ شَقَّا We brought down the rain and then we split the earth. And you can see it when the rain reaches and the seed it says اختَرَتَ بِهِ نَبَاتَ الْأَرْضِ.

When Allah splits that open, it causes the earth to break forth. So Allah is doing all that. And so He tells, فَلْيَنظُرَ الْإِنسَانِ Let a man look at his طَعَامِ رَطَعَامِ نَصَبَ بْنَمَا صَبَّةٍ مَّا شَقَقْنَا أَرْضَ شَقَّةً فَأَنْبَتَنَ بِهِ حَبًّا وَعِنَبًا وَقَبًّا وَزَيْتُونًا Right? All these things Allah has given, the inab, the grapes, the zaytoon, olives, the dates, all of these things, the fodder, the animals eat and we benefit from, secondarily, right? It's in there, abba, how we benefit from abba, benefited secondarily.

So that's Allah saying, look at those things, reflect on them and realize that's all from Allah subhanahu wa ta'ala. Why? Because He says here, وَذِكْرَ الْآيَةِ الْمُرْجِفَاتِ غَيْرِهَا Remember the other ayahs that should put fear in your hearts. إِنَّ اللَّهَ لَا يُغَيِّرُ مَا بِقَوْمٍ حَتِّي يُغَيِّرُ مَا بِأَنفُسِهِمْ Allah does not change the blessings of a people and turn them into the opposites until they change themselves by not showing Him gratitude for them. That's how that verse is interpreted by the scholar.

إِنَّ اللَّهَ لَا يُغَيِّرُ مَا بِقَوْمٍ Allah gives you a ni'ma and He won't take it away from you until you show ingratitude for it. Allah says, ذَلِكَ بِأَنَّ اللَّهَ لَمْ يَكُون مُغَيِّرًا مُغَيِّرًا نِعْمَةً أَنْعَمَهَا عَلَىٰ قَوْمٍ حَتَّى يُغَيِّرُ مَا بِأَنفُسِهِمْ That is because Allah will not change the blessing that He has given a people until they change themselves through ingratitude. And the poet said, إِن كُنْتَ فِي نِعْمَةٍ فَرْعَاهَا إِنَّ الْمَعَاصِي تَزِيرُ النِّعْمِ If you're in ni'ma, guard it because disobedience takes away your ni'ma.

If you're disobedient to Allah, you lose your ni'ma. And that's why Al-Waqi' when he was asked when he was young, when he was old he could move so quickly and somebody asked him the secret of that and he said, ثِرْكَ الْجَوَارِحْ حَفِظْنَاهَا لِلَّهِ فِي سِغْرِنَا فَحَافِظَهَا لَنَا فِي كِبْرِنَا These are limbs that we guarded for Allah's sake when we were young, and so Allah has guarded them for our sake as we're old. So if you protect the ni'ma that Allah has given you, then that's the way you maintain them.

قَيْدُ النِّعْمِ They say قَيْدُ النِّعْمِ الشُكَر That keeping the ni'ma with you is gratitude. تَأَذَّنَا رَبُّكَ لَإِن شَكَرْتُمْ لَأِزِيدَنَّكُمْ Allah has announced to us, He's told us, if you show gratitude for My ni'ma, I will increase you in them. I'll give you more.

So oblivion to ni'ma is a disaster. It's a disaster. And the other thing about this is what's known as استدراج.

And استدراج is that Allah subhanahu wa ta'ala will allow you to continue on in disobedience and increase you in blessings. And you think that it's because Allah likes you. And that's what the man says in the Qur'an when he says that Allah subhanahu wa ta'ala says that the man who He's given blessings and things, he says رَبِّي أَكْرَمَنِي My Lord, He's given me ikram.

And then فَإِذَا قَدَرْ عَرِزْقَهُ قَالَ إِنَّ رَبِّي أَهَانَنِي فَيَقُولُ رَبِّي أَهَانَنِي When Allah makes it constricted for him and difficult, he says, My Lord, أَهَانَنِي He's insulted me, He's denigrated me. So people see that if it's difficult, they think that that's a sign that Allah hates them. If it's easy, they think Allah loves them.

Now this is an important point because there's a few things happening. In terms of ni'mah, first, what is a ni'mah? In other words, how do we identify a ni'mah? If you look, there are things that will benefit you. They'll benefit you short term and long term.

An example of that would be like علم and حسن الخلق, good character. So things that benefit you in short term and long term, that is a true ni'mah. That is a true ni'mah.

Something that will benefit you in your dunya and your akhirah. That is pure, unadulterated ni'mah. And then you have something that is نافع.

You have something that is ضر في الحال والمآل. It harms you in the short term and the long term, like ignorance and bad character. سوء الخلق And then you have something that is beneficial in the short term, but in the long term it's not good for it.

And there's a lot of things like that. شهوات are generally like that. If you eat a whole bunch, you're enjoying it while you're eating, but then overeating is bad for you.

It's just bad for you, simple as that. So it's beneficial short term, long term it's harmful. And then there's other things the opposite of that.

They're harmful in the short term, beneficial in the long term, like suppressing the شهوات. So stopping before you're full, that's difficult. So يضرك في الحال, it's something you don't really like in the short term, but in the long term it's good for you.

And ignorant people see this as a ni'mah. Ignorant people see the short term benefit that has long term harm, they see it as a ni'mah, and they like it. And that's why they're not very intelligent.

Because people, intelligent people, the difference between intelligent people and ignorant people is intelligent people look at the عاقبة. They always look at the long term. Children can't look at long term.

And that was why one of those tests that they did on the EQ test, which I thought was really interesting, is the man who did all the studies on children that were successful later on in life. I mean they have different criteria for success than we do. But the point of the test was, is that they would take a child and say, I'll give you, they'd leave cookies out and they'd come in, oh can I have a cookie? And they'd say, I'll tell you what, you can either have one now or you can have two later.

And consistently the more intelligent ones would pick the two later. In other words, they were willing to put off the short term gain for a long term greater gain. And that is intelligent.

That is a sign of intelligence in Islam. العاقبة للمتقين The kuffar look short term, fourth quarter. The Muslims look long term.

Now, there's dunya, like for instance, one of the things about the Jews, is that the Jews are good at that. They're good at thinking long term benefit. And this is one of the problems with the Muslims now.

Muslims are very short sighted. Which is a sign of our own stupidity. We've lost the intelligence we had because we left guidance.

And if we seek dignity from other than Islam, Allah will humiliate us. You see, so it doesn't work for us. The rules are different for the Muslims and the kuffar.

Did I finish with this? Yeah. So, another thing about this is, there are two types of ni'ma. There are what are called usool al-ni'ma and furoo al-ni'ma.

Usool al-ni'ma are the roots of the ni'ma, which are the most important ones. And then there's furoo al-ni'ma. There's branches.

The usool al-ni'ma are things like health, iman, Islam, safety in your body, and where you live, and in your well-being, and those things. Those are the most important ones. And then there's other ones that are embellished.

Allah subhanahu wa ta'ala says about about Talut in the Quran, when the Bani Israel, you know, they say, how can he be a king? He doesn't have any money. And Allah subhanahu wa ta'ala, He says, inna Allah astataahu wazadahu bastatan fil ilmi wal jismi. Allah chose him, but not only that, He gave him bastaa.

He gave him increase in knowledge and in his body. So this is, this is something that Allah embellishes people with. And that's why Sayyidina Umar said, idha ba'athum rasoolan fa ba'athu hasan al-wajh.

If you send a messenger, send somebody with a good face. Right? Because you want somebody to present, in this culture, they call it personable person. Somebody who's, you see them and there's an automatic attraction to that person.

The reason for that is there's a wisdom in it, that Allah has inclined the hearts towards those people. And so that's why the messengers were all attractive to look at. Leadership, one of the qualities that leadership has is height.

Right? To be actually tall. That's something that many leaders have in history. Napoleon is a serious exception.

To that rule. But generally, people tend to follow, you know, with kids, the tall one will often be the leader of the gang and things like that. It'll either be the tall one or the clever one.

One man once said to the Prophet, in front of the Prophet, he asked for Allah, subhanahu wa ta'ala, Allahumma inni asaruka tamaman ni'ma Oh Allah, I ask you for the completion of ni'ma. That's completion of ni'ma. So, if you had a good life in this world, it's a ni'ma from Allah. And if you had iman, that's the greatest ni'ma in the dunya.

But the completion of that ni'ma is to enter into paradise with Allah, subhanahu wa ta'ala. Fadhkhuli fee ibadi, wadhkhuli jannati, enter amongst my servants into my paradise. That is the ni'ma.

And then Islam is the ni'ma of Allah that He gave us. Right? Wa atmamtum alaykum ni'mati I have completed my ni'ma of Islam. Alaykum.

Right? So, in this world, Islam is the ni'ma that Allah has given us. And then another thing that Sheikh Muhammad al-Hajjar, who I met in Medina, I was walking out with him to his car and he said, right next to the Bab as-Salam, he said, man asbaha mu'afan fee bednihi, aaminan fee sirbihi, anduhu qutu yawmihi, faka annama heezat dahu al-dunya bee hadhaafiriha. Whoever wakes up in a healthy state, mu'afa, right? He's in a good state in his body.

And he's safe in his possession, in his, what he has. And he has enough to get through the day. It's as if he has the world and everything in it.

Right? That's a hadith from Tirmidhi. So if you really reflect on that, if Allah, if you woke up this morning with aafiyah, with good health, and you're protected, you don't, you're not fearing anything, and you have enough just to get through this day, it's as if you have everything in the dunya. And so that's, that's a ni'ma that you should be, all of us should be grateful for.

So, branches would be things like ornamented ni'ma, blessings that, you know, for instance, a house, you need a place to live. But if you have extra things in the house, and furniture, and all those, those are like furu'a ni'ma. I mean it's, they're accessory ni'mas.

You don't really need them, they're more. And the more ni'ma you have, the more hisab you have. And that's why when the Prophet once went to a house and they had meat and dates and cool water, and Allah said, ثُمَّ لَتُسْأَلُونَ يَوْمَ إِذَا عَنِ النَّعِيمِ And then you would be asked about the ni'ma, the blessing.

And the Prophet said, هَذَهُوَ النَّعِيمِ This is the ni'ma we're gonna be asked about. So some of the sahaba came to the Prophet and they said, Ya Rasulullah, we realize we don't have any blessings to be asked about. Because they, they weren't eating meat and dates.

So, Jibreel alaihi salam told the Prophet that Allah wants to know, do they wear sandals and do they drink cold water? And the reasoning behind that is, is that you can walk without shoes. So that is a ni'ma to have shoes on your feet. And you can drink water that's not cold.

So if you drink cool water, the coolness in it is a ni'ma that you'll be asked about. And you can just continue on from there. To do, yeah, Sayyidina Ali, he said, أَعْقَلُ نَاسَ الزُهَادِ The most intelligent people are people that do without in this world.

Like the miser who won't spend even on himself, he just hoards the money, that is the stupidest person. Because he's not enjoying it in this world and it becomes a punishment for him in the next world. But the point is the zuhad, the zahid, and that's a maqam.

If the heart's not connected to it, in other words, if you have all this wealth and you could lose it tomorrow and you would be the same person, then that's a sign you're a zahid. Now, he says, رحمه الله that in order to treat the self or the heart itself, you have to prohibit the self from what it desires. And that's why the Prophet said, man ootia samta faqat ootia al-hikmah, whoever was given silence, just as part of his nature, he's been given wisdom, there's a wisdom in being silent.

Just in being silent. Even if he's an ignorant person, if he was given silence, he has a wisdom. Which is, if you're an ignorant person, don't make a fool out of yourself.

If you have something to say, then in that case, speech is better than silence. If you have nothing to say, then it's mubah, right? But there's no, there's no benefit in it, it's just mubah. Now, if that mubah kalam is how are you, where have you been, this and that, that's fine.

But if it's, did you hear about so and so, then it moves to either makruh or haram. And so, learning how to control the tongue is, that's a big discipline. Imam Shafi'i said that when he was in a gathering and needed to say something, he always looked into himself to make sure that he wasn't saying it out of a desire, from his nafs, to prove something or to show his knowledge.

He checked first that, is it acceptable to him that somebody else would say the same thing? And he said if he was aware of that, then he would say it. If he wasn't, he would remain silent, as a tarbiyah for his nafs. And he also said, that I never had a debate with anyone, except that I prayed to Allah, he made a dua, that Allah would make the truth manifest on his opponent's tongue, so that he could submit to it.

He didn't want to win the debate, he would actually prefer to lose the debate, so it would make him humble. And that's a rajab, that's a man. So, that's the point, is that, and the Prophet was the most quiet of people, when he spoke, he spoke the truth.

So, he was not somebody who was, what's called farfad, and he said, sallallahu alayhi wa sallam, inna ab'adukum minni wa ab'adukum ilayya yawm al-qiyamah, al-farfarin, al-mutafayhiqeen, al-farfarin, there's three things, al-farfarin, wal-mutafayhiqeen is the last one, and they knew the farfarin, and they knew the second one, they didn't know the mutafayhiqeen, he said it was mutakaddar. Farfad is somebody that speaks a lot, farfara, yutharfiru, they speak a lot. So, he didn't like people that just spoke all the time, right, chatterbox, what they call a chatterbox.

If you're teaching, if you're saying something useful, it's another thing, but if it's just kalaam, for kalaam's sake, then it's a waste of time. And Imam Marik, R.A., said about one man, one of his students, he said, ni'ma ar-rajal, illa annahu yatakadnamu kalaam ash-shahran fee yawmin wahid, he's a good man, except he speaks a month's worth of talking in one day. And then, al-fiqra wa huwa khali, fiqra means to, wa huwa khali means literally to be in a place where there's nobody around, and you're reflecting, and that's called meditation in English.

To meditate is a practice, right, not like a yogic or something like that, but literally sitting and meditating. And basically, there's different ways to do that, and you get into different traditions within the tradition of Islam, some will do al-tafakkar fil-mawt, like doing ahwal al-qiyamah, which is a practice, so you can literally, you sit and you go through the qiyamah, the visualization of going through the raising in the grave and these type of things. Other is to do tafakkar in the divine names, so you go through the divine names, right, ya allahu ya rahmanu ya rahimu, right, you go through the divine names and reflect on the attributes, things like that, right.

So, fiqra, wa huwa khali, is useful for the heart. And then, sohbat al-akhiyari ahl al-sutqi man yuhtada bihalihim wa n-nitqi, this is keeping the company of good people, and it comes from a Quranic injunction in which Allah says, ya ayyuha alladhina amanu, faqulla wa kunu ma'a s-sadiqin, have taqwa and be with the truthful one, in other words, sohba, and this is what they call in Persia, sohbat, you know, keeping good company with the righteous people in order to purify your heart. And Imam Ibn Aqayla, R.A. says in the Hikam, la tashaab man la yunhiduka halahu wa la yadulluka arallahi maqalahu, don't take as a companion somebody whose state does not elevate you, and his speech does not direct you to Allah.

And Sidi Ahmed Zarrukh says in his commentary, he's not talking about shuyukh, he's talking about your friends that you spend time with. In other words, you should find people that are going to elevate your state, not to bring it down. And by elevating your state, their state is elevated, so you become elevated.

What it means is, is that their hal with Allah is that they're in a good state, they're in a state of gratitude. So if you're with people in a state of gratitude, it's hard to complain around them. Right? You can't, because they're saying, alhamdulillah, subhanAllah, I feel really grateful, you know, subhanAllah, Allahu Akbar, Allah's given us so much, and so you're sitting there going, yeah, I'm sorry, I can't agree with you, you know, life's tough, and blah, blah, blah.

He's in a different hal from you. So, if you're the one in shukr, don't be with the one in shakwa. And if you're the one in shakwa, desire to be with the one in shukr, in order for you to become like him.

So don't bring him down, let him bring you up. That's what it's saying. So, maybe you should just try enumerating his blessings instead of looking at the troubles. So, you change, they help you change your focus and remind you. And it's amazing how a person's state can be changed by just sitting with somebody who gives them good and sincere advice.

Really, they can completely turn that heart around, and a person who is completely black and negative, because the effect of it was useful and valuable, they change their perspective and that's guidance. One of them, if you buy it, the only problem with it is it could disappear at any time.

But I have another house that's going to last forever, and it's guaranteed. But it's more expensive. So which one are you going to buy? Right? Seriously, which one are you going to buy? Any man with intellect or woman with intellect, they're going to buy the more expensive one.

And that's Akhira, and you buy it with your soul. It's for sale, you buy it with your soul. And the other one, you buy with your soul as well.

And then the best of Ibadah is Tafakkur, and the highest is what is called Al-Fana, and Al-Fana comes from Imam Al-Junaid, and Imam Al-Junaid called Al-Fana'u, which in Arabic literally means extinction.

And this comes from the idea that the nurse experiences separation. In other words, each one of us has an experience of separation from other people. And one of the things that you notice about a child is that a newborn does not have that experience.

There's no mass, there's no eye, there's no focus of the self. And you can see it because even the way they look around, there's no centralization. And what happens is they begin, as the child comes in to its identity, which is its eyeness, then it begins to see itself as separate.

Now, Imam Al-Junaid said that what happens in deep spiritual practice is that there can be experiences. One type is called Ahad, which Ahad is when somebody is overcome with a spiritual state that often is uncontrollable. I mean it is uncontrollable.

And the ulama of this science differentiate between Hal and Maqam. And Maqam is actually higher than Hal. The Maqam is where you're fixed in a state, a condition.

So Maqam of Tawbah is where you can't do Masiyah, because you're in the Maqam of Tawbah. And now you're in a Tawbah and you're in a Maqam now, and so you can't do things wrong-wittingly. The Hal of Tawbah is when, for instance, somebody is doing wrong actions and suddenly an intense state of remorse overcomes them and they ask Allah for forgiveness.

Right? So that's a Hal, but it's not a Maqam. And the Maqam is better because it continues. Maqam of Zuhr is when somebody is in a station.

Hal of Zuhr is a Hal that takes them, and it doesn't last. So a person might go into a Hal of Zuhr, or they might go into a Hal of Tawbah, but it doesn't last. So the Maqam is over the Hal.

And then you have what's called Awarid. And Awarid is an inrush that comes into the heart, very powerful, and it usually floods the heart with light. And there's an incredible expansion that can happen from that.

And that's also a temporary type state. What he's saying is that the highest, and there's something after Fana which is called Baqab. But imagine, I'd said, and Fana is when the self is no longer aware of itself.

In other words, there's a Reba that takes place. There's a loss of the experience of the self. And this does not last because if it lasts, you call it insanity.

Right? And that's why spiritual psychosis is real. There are people that actually go into experiences that they can't control or understand, and they get sick. And it happens all the time in this culture.

There's a lot of people that do meditation and go, like, get into Zen Buddhism or get in, and they start doing a lot of meditation, and they literally can lose it completely. And that's why it's so dangerous to be doing these things without the protection of prayer, without protection of wudu, without protection, because Islam is all grounding. We're not trying to get these spiritual experiences.

We're to be grounded. That's what Allah has commanded us to do, to be grounded. But if you do the thing which Allah commands you to do, and you do it properly, then often there are certain things that will happen interiorly in the inner self.

But what the Muslims recognize is that Allah subhanahu wa ta'ala, if a person is sincere and a person is remembering Allah subhanahu wa ta'ala, then Allah subhanahu wa ta'ala can put them in a state of absolute witnessing with Allah where they're not aware of their surroundings. And many, many of the sahaba, obviously, many of them were in that state.

And this is one of the interpretations of the hadith that my slave does not draw near to me by anything more beloved to me than the fall. And he will continue to draw near to me with the marafil until I love him. And when I love him, I become the eye with which he sees, the ear with which he hears, the tongue with which he speaks, the hand with which he grasps.

And if he seeks refuge in me, I give him refuge. If he asks me, I give him. That hadith never means that God becomes man, which is called huloo.

Huloo is haram to believe that. And if you believe that, you're outside of Islam. Huloo is the idea of incarnation.

And that hadith, it never means that. And anybody that believes that is a zindiq, they're heretic, out of Islam. So we don't believe in divine incarnation. 

We don't believe in that. We don't believe that God becomes creation. That's like Christian, Hindu, all those types.

But we do believe that the human being can be in such a state of awareness of the fi'al of Allah. Because there's a fi'al of Allah that is part of creation. In other words, it's actually when Allah, like the whole world is a fi'al of Allah.

But we don't say it's Allah. And there are people that go into a state of absolute witnessing, where they see everything being the fi'al of Allah. In other words, they're seeing Allah behind all of creation.

So they don't see otherness in the same way that other people are in this world of separation. And that's why they recognize that everything is from Allah. The good and the bad.

And so they see it as all good. And that's why, for the moment, everything is good. That's what that hadith means.

Even though there is good and bad, it's all from Allah. And because it's all from Allah, it has to be all good for the moment. Because even the bad, there's a wisdom in it for him to grow, to be purified and to draw nearer to Allah.

So he sees the bad even as good. And that's why Allah says, أَعْسَنْ تُحِبُّ شَيْئًا وَهُوَ شَرٌّ لَكُمْ Maybe you love a thing and it's bad for you. وَأَعْسَنْ تَكْرَهُ شَيْئًا وَهُوَ خَيْرٌ لَكُمْ And maybe you hate a thing, but it's good for you.

And Allah knows and you don't know. So that's the idea of being in the state of مشاهدة or مراقبة with Allah. Now what you have to do is just go through and you have that sheet in front of you.

And Qadi Abu Bakr Ibn Arabi said at the end of his book, Qanun At-Tawil, he said that usually, he said that Shaitan's first and foremost task for the Muslim is to get them away from the remembrance of Allah. And he begins it by Bismillah Ar-Rahman Ar-Rahim. And that comes from the Hadith that anything worth doing, if it does not begin with Bismillah Ar-Rahman Ar-Rahim, it's cut off from Barakah. So, anything that's worth doing, you should always do in the name of Allah, in the name of compassion.

And then he begins with Qul Huwa Allahu Ahad, three times. And this comes from a Hadith in which the Prophet Sallallahu Alaihi Wasallam said, Ayyajuzu Ahadukum Al-Yakhtamul Qur'ana Kullayoun. Is one of you incapable of reading the Qur'an every day, one time? And they said, who could do that, Ya Rasulullah? And they said, if you recite Ikhlas, Ikhlasu Adilu Firath Al-Qur'an.

It actually is the equivalent of one third of the Qur'an, in its weight and meaning. And so by reading it three times, we hope for the reward of reading the entire Qur'an. And so it should be read every day, preferably after every prayer, but at least in the morning and the evening.

And then the next two are called the Mu'awwilatain. And Mu'awwil means the thing that gives you refuge. Ta'weeda.

Ta'weeda. And Ta'weeda is protection. So the Mu'awwil gives you protection.

Umar Shafi'i considered it to be permissible and there was nothing in it. And he used the proofs from hadith that indicate that there was... There was a teacher and a group learning and he sat with the people learning and he said... I was sent a teacher. But he didn't make inkar out of the other group.

Because of the weakness of that hadith, it's hard to get a hukm on it. But there is another absolutely sound hadith which says that Allah has mala'ika sayyara, traveling angels, that look for hilaqa zikr. They're looking for the circles of zikr.

And when they come upon them, they descend on them. And then when they go back to Allah, Allah asks them, how did you find my servants? And then the hadith says... Allah knows better. He's just... this is to teach us.

And they said... We found them saying... And it says that they were in a halaqa. Now, some of the ulema said it's permissible to come together but not to do it on one voice. Because the hadith does not indicate that there was one voice.

Other of the ulema said, there's no prohibition of doing it on one voice. And zikr is permissible. And so they don't see that there should be any prohibition.

Because there are definitely hadith that indicate that they did say things together. Like takbir and things like that. So you're gonna get khilafi issues.

The majority of the scholars, particularly the later scholars, permitted it. The mu'hideen actually encouraged it in Morocco. And they actually encouraged reading the Quran from one voice together.

And they created a sunnah in Morocco that continues after this day. Which was having the Quran completed every month in the masjid of Morocco. And they do that to this day.

And the mu'hideen started that. And the reason they did it is because they said people have stopped reading the Quran. And if we have a huzb in the masjid, then there'll be many people that will read it just because it's there and it's a good thing to do.

So it's like encouraging them to do it. And so they said it was better that people read and do khatm of the Quran, than that they don't read it. Ibn al-Hajj disagreed with them in his madkhal.

Um, inshallah, a couple of, one, I want to, this, cause this is the last class, um, for people that are going on the, uh, intensive. Jazakumullah khairan. And tofeeq, inshallah.

And I want to thank all of you, uh, for the attendance, and, and the, the respect, and, and, and the, uh, just the general good spirit that's been, uh, present throughout the whole, uh, course. And, uh, you know, excuse me for any shortcomings, or, uh, any breaches of courtesy. Um, and make du'a also.

Bismillah ar-Rahman ar-Rahim. By the blessing of Allah subhanahu wa ta'ala, we were able to go through this text to complete it. And we ask Allah subhanahu wa ta'ala, inshallah, to give us the ability to implement what we, what we've learned.

And in ending this text, I would like to give counsel that was written by one of our great scholars, Sidi Ahmed Zarouk, who died in 1492. He was a great scholar from Morocco, and he died in Masada in Libya. And one of the things that he said is that the fragrance of his teaching would disseminate in the world, or diffuse in the world, long after his bones were laid to rest.

And so it's, it's one of his really karamat or, or, uh, saintly miracles that his teaching is still affecting us and influencing us. He said, Allah subhanahu wa ta'ala anhum, Know, may Allah give you and us success, and rectify our worldly and otherworldly lives, and grant us adherence to the way of the truth in our journeys and in our sojourns, that repentance is a key, and taqwa is vast, and uprightness is the source of rectification. Furthermore, a servant is never free of either blunders or shortcomings or lassitude.

Therefore, never be neglectful of tawbah, repentance, and never turn away from the act of returning to Allah subhanahu wa ta'ala, and never neglect acts that bring you closer to Allah subhanahu wa ta'ala. Indeed, every time one of these three occurs, repent and return. Every time that you make a mistake, listen and obey.

Any time you display shortcomings or show lack of enthusiasm, don't desist in your efforts. Let your main concern be to remove from your outer state anything that is displeasing, and then maintain its outward state through continuous counsel. Continue doing this until you find that you are fleeing from anything outwardly displeasing or second nature, and that your avoidance of the boundaries of prohibited things is as if it acts like a protective net that is placed before you.

At this point, it is time to turn inward toward your heart's presence and to its reality with both reflection and remembrance. Don't hasten the end result before you have completed the beginning, but likewise, don't begin without looking toward the end result. This is so because the one who seeks the outset at the end loses providential care, and the one who seeks the end at the outset loses providential guidance.

Act in accordance with principles and the appropriate legal rulings, and not in accordance with stories and fantasies. Don't even consider stories of how things went with others, except as a tonic to strengthen your resolve. Certainly not as a reference based upon their outward forms or what they seem to be telling us.

In all of this, depend upon a clear path you can refer to and a foundation that you can depend upon in all of your states. The best of these is the path of Ibn Ata'illah, given that in it is a clear direction to Allah. Do not take from others words unless it is in accordance with your own path, but submit to their implications if you desire realization.

Avoid all forms of vain and foul speech to your absolute utmost. Put aside anything that you cannot discern, its benefit immediately. Beware of being extremely hard on yourself before you have obtained a mastery over it, but also beware of being too lax with it in anything that concerns sacred rulings.

This is because it is constantly fleeing from moderation in everything, and it inclines towards extremism in both matters of deviance and guidance. To repeat, this is so because it is constantly fleeing from moderation in everything, and it inclines towards extremism in both matters of deviance and guidance. So, just to stop and look, this is extremely important because what the Shaykh is telling us is that the nature of the self is extremism, and he is telling us to be aware of this in both good deeds and in foul deeds, in both obedience to Allah subhanahu wa ta'ala and disobedience to Allah subhanahu wa ta'ala.

So he is telling us, beware of being extremely hard on yourself before you have obtained a mastery over it, but also beware of being too lax with it in anything that concerns sacred rulings. He continues, Seek out a companion to help you out in your affairs and take his counsel concerning matters that occur from both your inward states and your outward affairs. If you do indeed take his companionship, then treat him in a manner commensurate with his state and give him of your counsel based upon his inabilities and abilities, because the perfected friend is no longer to be found.

Indeed, in these times, even a suitable companion who is agreeable rarely lasts. And beware of the majority of people in matters that concern your religious and your worldly states, unless you have ascertained he has some sound relationship with his Lord based upon a knowledge that is free of his caprice or love of leadership and a sound intellect free of the pitfalls of hidden agendas. Do not be heedless of the machinations of others or their hidden states.

Consider these two from both their origins and their actions. A person of character and family distinction rarely affects you with other than good, and yet a person of low origins' roots usually cause him to disregard you when times get tough. Be extremely vigilant of the dominant qualities of a given people in any given land and don't be heedless of the divine wisdom in the creation and notice gatheredness and separation.

Some of this we have already covered in our book Al-Qara'ib, so take a look at it there. Only have time in a matter appropriate to the time's specific needs using gentleness and toleration and be very wary of either harshness or laxity. This is so because too much laxity concerning permissible matters pulls the heart backward in its journey until even a man of resolve ends up looking like a foolish boy.

Work for this world as if you will live forever, but work for your next life as if you die tomorrow. Thus, do not neglect the externals of your worldly needs. In the meantime, do not be heedless of your end and final resting place.

Be extremely vigilant about avoiding positions of leadership, but should you be tried with such matters, at least know your own limitations. Be absolutely sincere to allow the sincerity of one who knows full well who is placing demands upon him. Surrender completely to his decree with the submission of one who knows he can never overcome him.

Have a firm foundation for all of your affairs and you will be safe from their pitfalls. Organize your devotional practices and you will find your time is extended due to the barakah or blessings in it. Never be fanatical about anything, whether it is the truth or whether it is false, for your heart will then remain in a state of soundness towards others.

Never claim anything you are entitled to, not to mention what you are not entitled to, and you will be safe from tricks and treachery. This is so because anyone who claims some rank above his own will fall in humiliation, whereas those who claim a rank they warrant will have it stripped from them, while those who claim a station less than their true rank will be elevated to even higher levels than they actually deserve. Never give your companion anything of your state other than what his own state warrants.

This is so because if you go down to his level, he will show you contempt, whereas if you attempt to raise him up to your level, he will abandon you. Never demand a right from anyone, whether an intimate or a stranger. The reason for this is a stranger in reality owes you nothing, and someone close to you is too precious to direct your blame to him.

Never assume that anyone in this world can really understand your circumstances other than from the perspective of his own circumstances. This is so because in reality, everyone only sees things in accordance with their frames of reference and their personal path. However, when aims, purposes, and aspirations are similar, people tend to work together toward a common goal.

Never belittle any talk that involves absent people, even if there is no harm in it, due to the possibility of harm entering into it. Guard your secrets even if you feel safe with someone, because the one you divulge your secret to is not a safer place than your own heart from whence it emanated. Never leave an atom's weight of your regular devotional practice.

Never be lenient with yourself in either lax times or times of high resolve. Indeed, should you miss some of your practice at a given time, redress it in another time. If you are not able to do your usual practice, at least occupy yourself with something else similar.

Never obey your ego, even for a moment. Never believe any of its claims, no matter what it tells you. Be vigilant about your resolve in all of your affairs to your utmost.

In fact, should you resolve to do something, then do it immediately before the resolve wanes. Examine your soul constantly in matters that you are obliged to do or are needed to be done. Anything you are in no need of doing, leave it, even if it is something that is recommended.

That means not involving yourself in anything other than absolutely necessary things, and real discernible needs. Treat others just as you would want to be treated, and fulfill to them what is due to them. All of this is really epitomized in the words of the poet when he said, If you desire to live such that your religion is safe, and your portion is full, and your honor is sound, guard your tongue, and never mention another's faults, remembering you yourself have faults, and others have tongues.

Watch your eye. Should it ever reveal to you the faults of others, say to it, O my eye, other people have eyes too. Live treating others well, and avoid aggression, and should others aggress against you, leave them, but in the best way.

The source of these words is in fact nothing other than the traditions of the Prophet, when he said, Be vigilant of Allah wherever you are, and follow a misdeed with a good deed, and it will remove it, and treat others with the most excellent of character. In another he said, Peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, Every child of Adam makes mistakes, and the best of those who make mistakes are those who seek to redress them. Again, the Holy Spirit inspired my heart's core, that no soul will die until it fulfills its decreed portion of this world, and its appointed time here.

So be conscious of Allah, and make your requests with dignity. In summation, repentance, awareness of Allah, and uprightness are the foundations of all that is beneficial. The truth is clear, and its details are weighty and significant.

The affair belongs only to Allah, success is in His hands. Peace be upon you.
